Competitive advantages 
High entry barriers to the segment 
Comprehensive product portfolio, empowered by subsidiaries 
Acter provides a one-stop EPCM solution, leveraging its subsidiaries to deliver complete 
solutions. Within the group, the parent company Acter focuses on overall cleanroom and 
MEP system integration; subsidiary Novatech provides primary chemical systems and 
hook-up systems; and sub-subsidiary Rayzer specialises in primary specialty-gas systems 
and hook-up systems. In China, Suzhou Winmax, also a subsidiary of Novatech, focuses 
on the supply of wet processing and chemical handling equipment. This integrated model 
covers cleanroom engineering integration, water, power, chemical and gas-utility systems, 
as well as post-completion validation and maintenance, ensuring cross-system 
compatibility while mitigating risks associated with multi-contractor hiring.

Under global supply chain diversification trends, this unified group strategy strengthens 
order-winning capabilities in overseas markets. Clients expanding internationally face 
regulatory, linguistic and supply-chain complexities, favouring partners that offer one-stop 
services with a proven cross-border track record.

A diverse client base helps Acter weather economic cycles 
Acter’s main competitive advantage is its diversification of clients by industry and region. 
This guides the company through the peaks and troughs of the economy, which is 
especially crucial given the strong cyclicality of the semiconductor/high-tech industry.  Acter 
serves a broad client base across semiconductors, OSAT, PCB, electronics assembly 
(ODM), CSP data centres, biopharmaceuticals and even the consumer industry (including 
commercial real estate and logistics), whereas many peers rely on a few large foundry 
clients. When semiconductor demand slows, capacity expansion in the PCB or data centre 
sectors offset the decline. Additionally, Acter avoids concentration risk and margin 
compression from major foundry accounts, as projects from leading foundries yield lower 
margins; by serving niche markets like US-based CSPs, the company achieves higher 
gross margins.

Geographically, Acter operates across Taiwan, China, Southeast Asia, the US and other 
countries. The company has OSAT and foundry customers in Taiwan and China, PCB 
clients expanding into Thailand, and ODM and data centre clients establishing operations 
in the US. Across different countries, local hiring and local supply chain implementation 
can further reduce costs and increase efficiency relative to peers reliant on a cross-border 
labour force.

Labour shortages 
Amid global labour shortages, Acter minimises the operational impact and turns workforce 
constraints into a moat through localised talent development. Since engineering labour 
cannot easily be automated, Acter has built a cross-border talent pipeline via industry-
academia partnerships. The company recruits Southeast Asian students for specialised 
M&E engineering training in Taiwan, deploying them to global project sites after their 
graduation to solve labour bottlenecks. This structure maintains an engineering team with 
an average tenure of more than 7 years, creating an operational barrier in an industry with 
a high employee turnover rate.

Compared with peers relying on high-cost personnel sent from Taiwan, we note that Acter’s 
local hiring strategy creates cost and efficiency advantages. Prioritising compliant local 
hiring across overseas markets reduces travel expenses while accelerating integration with 
local supply chains and regulations. Localised teams also handle post-completion services 
and technical support directly on-site, building long-term client trust in overseas markets.

The supply of cleanroom 
services is limited due to 
high entry barriers in the 
segment

### Page 11

11

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Numerous competitors, each with different specialisation 
Acter faces different competitors across its main product lines. In the EPCM segment, 
including cleanroom and MEP works, key competitors include UIS (2404 TT, not rated), 
L&K (6139 TT, not rated), Yankey (6691 TT, not rated) and Jiuhan (6903 TT, not rated). 
Despite having business models similar to Acter, UIS, Yankey and Jiuhan focus more on 
major semiconductor players in both Taiwan and the US, and L&K concentrates on ASEAN 
semiconductor projects. In the equipment/material sales and agency space, Acter mainly 
competes with Marketech (6196 TT, not rated). In the water, gas and chemical delivery 
systems area, through Novatech and Rayzher, Acter group competes with Marketech, 
Mega Union (6944 TT, not rated), Taiwan Puritic (6826 TT, not rated), Trusval (6667 TT, not 
rated) and Cica-Huntek (6725 TT, not rated).

In our view, despite the competitive environment, Acter maintains a formidable competitive 
moat built on several key advantages. We attribute this to its more than 45 year experience 
in the industry, enabling it to develop specialised expertise that is hard for competitors to 
replicate.

Financial analysis 
We forecast the company’s net profit to rise at a CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E, driven by 
a revenue CAGR of 24.6% due to surging demand from the high-tech industry (an increase 
in the order backlog to TWD66bn at the end of July 2026 vs. TWD46bn at end-2025) and a 
3.3pp expansion in operating margin over 2026-28E, driven by the gross margin 
improvement from more favourable project wins and operating leverage.

Strong order visibility, with revenue expected to grow 
across all sectors over 2026-28E 
We forecast Acter’s revenue to increase at a CAGR of 24.6% over 2026-28E, driven by 
scale expansion and a growing demand from various industries, including semiconductor, 
electronic assembly, optoelectronics and others.

Acter: revenue growth trend

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Gross- and operating-margin expansion trend  
Over 2026-28E, we forecast Acter’s gross and operating margins to expand on rising 
revenue scale and a higher complexity of cleanrooms for advanced manufacturing. We 
note that the gross margin from high-tech cleanrooms is higher than that from consumer 
industries, such as commercial buildings and biopharmaceuticals. Thus, we estimate the 
company’s gross margin to reach 26.0% in 2028E from 18.9% in 2025 and its operating 
margin to reach 21.6% in 2028E from 13.7% in 2025.

(from TWD6,325m in 2026E). We expect its net margin to come in at 13.1% in 2027E and 
14.2% in 2028E (vs. 11.7% in 2026E) and for its net profit to grow at a CAGR of 36.9% 
over 2026-28E.

Intact demand to support revenue growth over our

We forecast Acter’s total cash and equivalents to reach TWD16,838m (36.8% of its total 
assets) in 2026E and increase to TWD27,839m in 2028E (42.8% of total assets) with net

free cash flow (FCF) to improve to TWD12,129m in 2028E, from TWD5,763m in 2026E. 
We believe the capacity expansion cost will initially be low and that the company will 
continue to benefit from the better profitability.

We expect Acter’s ROE to improve to a record-high of 56.8% in 2028E from 29.3% in 2025 
(following steady annual increases to 46.8% in 2026E and 54.9% in 2027E), which we 
attribute to margin expansion from the revenue size increase and gross margin expansion 
led by increases in cleanroom complexity.

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Valuation and risks  
Undemanding valuation, alongside its leading global 
positions in the OSAT and memory segments and an 
optimistic industry outlook  
We initiate coverage of Acter with a Buy (1) rating and 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based 
on a target PER of 20x applied to our one-year-forward EPS forecast. This target multiple 
is higher than the average of its past-3-year 1-year-forward PER of 12.2x (ranged from 
7.4x to 23.5x) and the average of its past-5-year 1-year-forward PER of 9.6x (ranged from 
4.4x to 23.5x), which we view as undemanding as we forecast a 36.9% net profit CAGR 
over 2026-28E, implying a 0.54x PEG. This is supported by its business expansion in the 
next 2-3 years. We see potential for a rerating of the stock, given Acter’s global leadership 
in cleanroom EPCM, particularly in the OSAT and memory industries.

The stock is trading currently at a 15.8x 1-year-forward PER vs. its earnings CAGR of 
36.9% over 2026-28E, translating to a 0.43x PEG ratio. Its share price has underperformed 
TAIEX Index by 32.7% in 3Q26, mainly due to its weaker-than-expected 2Q26 results. 
However, given the potential improvement in sector sentiment following TSMC’s results on 
15 October 2026, along with the likelihood that Acter’s revenue momentum will strengthen 
in the coming months, we recommend that investors buy the stock.

Acter: past-3-year-forward PER (average: 12.0x)    Acter: past-5-year-forward PER (average: 9.5x)

Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa estimates  Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa estimates

We compare Acter with other leading cleanroom EPCM companies such as UIS (2404 TT, 
TWD1,330.0, not rated), L&K (6139 TT, TWD787.0, not rated) and Yankey (6691 TT, 
TWD776.0, not rated). UIS, L&K and Yankey are trading at a PER of 14.7x (earnings 
CAGR of 40.7% over 2025-27E), 9.5x (earnings CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E) and 
15.1x (earnings CAGR of 53.6% over 2025-27E), on their 1-year-forward EPS, 
respectively.

Acter’s peers are trading at 18.7x for 2026E and 14.2x for 2027E. We forecast Acter to 
deliver an EPS CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E vs. 31.5% CAGR for the segment over the 
same period.

We believe our target one-year-forward PER of 20x (equivalent to a 0.54x PEG using 
2026-28E EPS CAGR) is undemanding as: 1) Acter is ramping up capacity to meet 
growing demand from high-tech customers and is in an expansion cycle; and 2) its 
leading position among OSAT and memory clients warrants a valuation premium, 
compared to its global peers.

Risks to our call  
Primary/key risks: 
Weaker-than-expected expansion in semiconductor industry 
The biggest downside risk to our call for Acter is weaker-than-expected expansion from 
semiconductor clients, especially in advanced-node foundries, advanced packaging and 
OSAT. If global demand for AI servers, HPC or HBM slows, or if clients delay fab build-ups 
and overseas expansion plans, it will directly pressure order inflows and backlog 
conversion for Acter.

Project execution delays or cancellations 
Acter’s revenue recognition is tied to large, complex EPCM and integrated engineering 
projects, which are often subject to customer-driven delays. Any postponement in project 
Our 12-month TP of 
TWD1,415 is also 
supported by our DCF 
valuation

### Page 18

18

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
schedule, construction bottlenecks or cancellation of large projects could defer revenue 
recognition and hurt overall profit. This risk is more obvious in overseas projects, where 
licensing, fire-safety approvals and hazardous-chemical regulations are more complicated.

Higher-than-expected raw-material price hikes 
Raw-material costs (especially copper and oil-related costs) account for 50-60% of COGS 
and thus the price volatility makes costs difficult to manage. Acter needs to maintain 
supplier relations, negotiate appropriate price-adjustment mechanisms with clients, and 
invest in R&D to identify composite material substitutes, thereby reducing reliance on any 
single component.

Secondary risks:  
Labour issues or larger-than-expected increases in labour costs  
Cleanroom engineering is highly labour-intensive and relies on on-site installation and 
supervision. Although Acter has built a local hiring and talent-development model, a larger-
than-expected shortage of engineers or rising wage pressure could still raise costs and 
constrain capacity expansion. Any inability to secure enough skilled labour may also limit 
the company’s ability to convert orders into revenue in time.

Margin pressure from lower-margin projects or mix shift 
Acter’s business mix includes a few low-margin C&S services as well as larger EPCM and 
system integration contracts. If the company takes on a higher proportion of lower-margin 
projects, or if competition intensifies in certain geographies or customer segments, gross 
margin could come under pressure. In addition, an unfavourable product mix shift towards 
less profitable work could offset benefits from backlog growth.

Overseas expansion and regulatory risks 
Acter is expanding across markets in the US, Southeast Asia, Japan and China, which 
exposes it to FX movements, local compliance requirements, tax complexity and regulatory 
uncertainty. Delays in licensing, changes in safety standards or local-content requirements 
could increase project costs and reduce execution efficiency. Any misstep in managing 
overseas operations could damage profitability and client retention. 
Key risks to our call: 
weaker-than-expected 
expansion in 
semiconductor industry, 
project delays or 
cancellations, and 
higher-than-expected 
raw-material price hikes

Company background 
History 
Acter (5536 TT), founded in 1979 and headquartered in Taichung, Taiwan, is a cleanroom 
EPCM provider with over four decades of experience. Starting with early mechanical and 
electrical (M&E) engineering through vertical and horizontal integration, Acter has now 
developed into an engineering technology group with cross-border execution capabilities. A 
cleanroom building process begins with civil & structural (C&S) works to establish the 
physical plant, followed by mechanical, electrical, and plumbing (MEP) works for 
essential utility generation, where Acter is one of the experts. After MEP is finished, 
chemicals/gas/water are then distributed via main systems through primary piping 
networks, and finally delivered to production equipment through hook-up systems. The 
company’s core competency is providing one-stop integrated total solutions, spanning 
services, primary piping networks and hook-up systems, leaving C&S services to other 
engineering peers, given the nature of the latter segment’s low margin and long timespan.

In terms of business model, Acter delivers EPCM (Engineering, Procurement, 
Construction, Management) capabilities. From initial customised planning and feasibility 
assessments to strategic procurement, installation, and post-completion maintenance, 
Acter builds production environments tailored to client process requirements.

Acter: EPCM solutions offered

Source: Company, Daiwa

Regarding global expansion, Acter actively responds to the global supply chain’s “N+1” 
strategy, operating across 11 regions with 33 companies globally. Its service network spans 
Taiwan, China, Southeast Asia (including Thailand, Malaysia, Vietnam, Indonesia, and 
Singapore), Japan, India, and the US. The company leverages a group synergy strategy 
by integrating resources from key subsidiaries – such as Novatech (6613 TT, not rated; 
provides chemical primary and hook-up systems), Rayzher (7703 TT, not rated; provides 
special gas primary and hook-up supply systems), and Acter Group (603163 CH, not rated; 
provides cleanroom engineering in China) – to form a complete technical ecosystem, 
ensuring cross-border capability and quality.

In 2Q26, Acter classified revenue as: cleanroom electromechanical (63%), water, gas and 
chemical delivery systems (28%), consumer industry electromechanical (6%), biomedical 
(2%) and high-tech equipment & materials sales & services (1%). In 2025, the product mix 
was: cleanroom electromechanical (71%), water, gas and chemical delivery systems 
(20%), consumer industry electromechanical (4%), biomedical (2%) and high-tech 
equipment & materials sales & services (3%).

Acter serves a diversified client base divided into high-tech and consumer divisions. The 
high-tech division focuses on advanced sectors including semiconductors, OSAT, PCB, AI 
data centres (for CSPs), and electronics assembly. The consumer division extends into 
healthcare, premium commercial properties, logistics facilities, and green engineering. This 
multi-industry strategy allows the company to capitalise on tech sector upgrades while 
maintaining resilient operations across technology (especially semiconductor) cycles.

Acter adopts a management strategy involving local hiring in overseas markets, prioritising 
collaboration with local supply chains and compliance with local regulations to reduce 
cross-border deployment costs and thus build strong local teams. Acter has more than 
2,600 employees worldwide (average age: 42 years; average length of service: 7 years). 
With more than 45 years of experience in EPCM, the company plays a reliable role for both 
high-tech and consumer industry.

neutrality while expanding capacity. Backed by solid corporate governance, Acter

Glossary of terms 
EPCM (engineering, procurement, construction, and management): Cleanroom 
construction services that cover the whole process including project design, purchasing, 
construction and management.

BIM (building information modelling): A digital modelling system of a building and its key 
information used in cleanroom construction.

C&S (civil & structural): Site infrastructure and the design and construction of load-
bearing elements.

MEP (mechanical, electrical, and plumbing): Building essential systems for environment 
control, power, lighting, water, and drainage.

ESG analysis

Update Date: 7 Oct 2026 
Source:  Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Acter maintains a structured governance framework that supports disciplined management. The
chairman is Chin-Li Liang. The company operates with defined responsibility across the board,
management, cyber security oversight, operations, and audit functions. It has established an
organisation function led by the President, a Chief Cyber Security Officer, a Response Team, an
Operations and Policy Enforcement Team, and an Audit Team to supervise policy execution and
review security implementation.
Capital management 1
Acter manages capital in a stable and shareholder-friendly manner. Over the past several years,
Acter has typically maintained a dividend payout ratio of 65-80%. It also maintains a strong
financing network across multiple banks, supporting flexibility for project execution and
expansion while preserving financial stability.
Related party &
transaction 1
Acter’s business is supported by a transparent network of suppliers, project partners, and
financial counterparties, including companies with equity ties and reciprocal business
relationships that are common in its operating ecosystem. These relationships are managed
through formal internal procedures for fund lending, endorsements and guarantees, and asset
acquisition and disposal, all of which are approved through shareholder mechanisms and
company rules. Such a structure helps keep inter-company and supply-chain transactions
organised and traceable, reducing the risk of unclear dealings.
E Product quality &safety 1
Acter executes its projects through a complete, disciplined process covering bidding
assessment, project planning, design, procurement, construction management, verification and
testing, completion and acceptance, closing review, and warranty service. Its cleanroom and
electromechanical engineering business is built around maintaining temperature, humidity, and
dust-free environments that support high-precision manufacturing. The company strengthens
quality control through BIM, Revit, simulation software, ISO drawing output tools, and automated
modeling, which improve design accuracy, construction efficiency, and project consistency.
E Ecological impacts 1
Acter has built a concrete sustainability programme with measurable environmental outcomes.
The company completed Scope 1, 2, and 3 greenhouse gas emissions calculation, obtained
third-party verification under ISO 14064-1:2018, and publicly disclosed energy consumption and
emissions data. It also implemented a strong waste reduction programme, achieving a 95%
wastepaper recycling rate at headquarters in 2025 and 100% paper recycling at the 19F and 20F
floors after establishing paper segregation and reuse procedures. The company also promoted
sustainability measures through emissions inventory management, environmental disclosures,
and resource-efficiency practices. These actions show active environmental management and a
clear commitment to lowering operational impact.

## Document 228: Eurozone _ Growth on track despite the turbulence (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone _ Growth on track despite the turbulence (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

8
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
GROWTH ON TRACK DESPITE THE TURBULENCE
Growth was stronger than expected in Q2 and is set to strengthen further in the coming quarters. According to our fore-
casts, it will reach 1.5% in 2027 after 1.1% in 2026. This growth is expected to be driven by investment and its knock-on
effect on exports. On an annual average basis, inflation is expected to stand at 3% in 2026 and 2.8% in 2027, peaking at

the end of 2026. The ECB is expected to continue its monetary tightening. At this stage, a single further rate rise, aimed at
keeping inflation expectations anchored, is expected in December. The impact of rising interest rates (key policy rates and
market rates) on public finances and private sector borrowing conditions will be worth monitoring.
GROWTH: BEYOND THE SHOCKS, A RESILIENT MOMENTUM
Growth in the Eurozone remained robust in the first half of 2026, at

0.25% q/q in Q1 and 0.33% q/q in Q2, excluding Ireland (where economic
activity is volatile). It was driven by Germany, where signs of a recovery
are becoming clearer (see Germany). Our baseline scenario forecasts
growth of 1.1% in 2026, followed by 1.5% in 2027 on an annual average
basis. This growth is expected to be driven by the strengthening of
trends already underway in public and private investment (particularly
in defence and AI) and in exports (trade in services and intra-zone trade

in goods). Household consumption is likely to be resilient too. According
to our forecasts, job creation would continue to underpin household
incomes, thereby cushioning the impact of inflation.
A NEW LEASE OF LIFE FOR INDUSTRY
The Eurozone is undergoing one of the most profound industrial trans-
formations in its history. This is giving rise to divergences between key
sectors but, above all, to new drivers of growth whose effects are beco-
ming increasingly apparent (see our analysis). Indeed, the signs of an

economic recovery are growing stronger. Business sentiment indicators
show a marked improvement in the economic outlook across most sec-
tors (Chart 2), driven primarily by electronics (AI, digitalisation), the
automotive sector and aerospace. European industry is thus benefi -
ting from strong demand for capital goods; a structural trend that will
continue to be fuelled by ongoing investment cycles in AI, defence and
electrification. The introduction of minimum European content quotas

in the draft law on the industrial accelerator, together with the Com -
mission’s intention to tighten the criteria for access to European public
procurement markets, will provide additional support for European in-
dustry in the longer term.
FOREIGN TRADE: A REPOSITIONING THAT IS PAYING OFF
Following a decline in 2026, linked to rising energy import costs, the
trade surplus is expected to rise to around 3.0% of GDP in 2027 (of
which 2% of GDP for goods and 1% for services). Growth in European ex-

ports has slowed compared with its pre-Covid trend and is being ham-
pered by the decline in Chinese and US markets. However, Eurozone
exports in volume rose significantly in Q2 (+3.4% q/q).
More importantly, since Liberation Day (April 2025), Eurozone coun -
tries have found significant sources of growth amongst other European
partners, which has enabled them to cushion the decline in exports to
certain third markets. This is evident in exports to the rest of the EU,

EFTA countries (Switzerland in particular) and, to a lesser extent, the
United Kingdom, Ukraine and the Balkans (Chart 3). The momentum of
intra-European trade therefore remains very strong. It is expected to
strengthen further with the anticipated investments in defence, AI and
electrification.
FINANCING: FINANCIAL CONDITIONS ARE EXPECTED TO REMAIN TIGHT
Financial conditions in the Eurozone remained tight in Q2 2026, accor-
ding to indices calculated by the ECB (including the BIG [Broad Inter -

mediation Gauge]). They are expected to tighten more significantly in
the second half of the year: i/ the rise in long-term bond yields, which
has accelerated since the outbreak of the conflict in Iran, is expected to
continue; and ii/ the ECB will continue its monetary tightening (+25 bps

The bank
for a changing
world
in Q3, then in Q4). For the time being, the debt burden is still weighing
only moderately on public finances due to the long average maturity of
countries’ debt (8.2 years on average in the Eurozone in July), but this
burden will increase by 2030.
On the corporate front, the rise in interest rates has remained contained
(+2 bps since January 2026, reaching 3.9% in July), which has contri -

buted to the strong growth in new investment loans (cumulated over
one year, +9.1% y/y in July compared with 15.3% y/y in September 2025).
By contrast, cumulative net debt securities issues over one year re -
mained stable in July, whilst cumulative net issues of listed shares over
one year halved month on month.
Rising interest rates will continue to hamper new variable-rate
home loans to households (around 65% of new lending) more signi -
ficantly than fixed-rate loans, for which rates stood in July at 3.69%

(+4 bps m/m) and 3.36% (+2 bps m/m) respectively. New home loans,
which are stable in 2026 on a cumulative over one year basis, are there-
fore unlikely to see any improvement. A fall in real-estate transactions
could weigh on new consumer credit through a reduction in expenditure
on home furnishings and renovations, whereas consumer credit has
been fairly buoyant so far (year-to-date, +5.2% y/y in July compared
with +1.4% y/y in January).
LABOUR MARKET: RESILIENCE YET TO BE CONFIRMED

Employment in the Eurozone rose again by an average of 0.1% q/q in
the first half of 2026, driven by Spain and Portugal. Given the growth
outlook in these two countries, this momentum is likely to continue,
whilst business sentiment surveys in Germany suggest that employ -
ment is beginning to stabilise. Nevertheless, employment levels are
plateauing (France, the Netherlands) or even falling in other countries
(Austria, Finland), which is likely to slow the decline in the Eurozone

unemployment rate, meaning it is expected to remain close to current
levels in 2027. Furthermore, second-round effects of the rise in inflation
on wages cannot be ruled out: the ECB’s wage tracker suggests wage
growth of close to 3% by summer 2027 (2.9% y/y in June 2027, compared
with 2.6% in September).
INFLATION: A PEAK EXPECTED IN LATE 2026
Changes in wage dynamics will be a key factor in the inflation out -
look for 2027, whilst energy price rises are picking up again, driven

by the conflict in the Middle East. This rise is even sharper than in
the spring for gas (linked to restocking ahead of winter) and is likely
to feed through to electricity prices in several countries. We therefore
anticipate inflation peaking at 3.7% y/y in Q4 2026, before easing as the
energy component falls. On an annual average, headline inflation is
expected to stand at 3% in 2026 and then at 2.8% in 2027.
At this stage, the pass-through of rising energy prices to other com -

ponents of the consumer price index, particularly services, remains li -
mited. The alternative core indicators monitored by the ECB, including
the PCCI (Persistent and Common Component of Inflation, 2.1% in July),
remain close to the 2% target. Core inflation, however, is expected to
peak two months later than headline inflation (3% in March 2027).
Inflationary risks are nevertheless tilted to the upside. These are lin -
ked to food prices, which are expected to be affected by the energy

shock and weather conditions (summer drought, El Niño); they could
also stem from a delayed impact of energy price rises on underlying
inflation. The rise in prices of energy-dependent inputs (notably che -
micals and plastics) and those linked to the AI sector is expected to be
sustained. Growth, which we expect to remain robust over the coming
quarters, will also fuel underlying inflationary pressures.
MONETARY POLICY: TIGHTENING WITHOUT SLOWING (GROWTH)

Following two 25-basis-point rises (in June and September), we ex -
pect a third in December, which would bring the deposit facility rate
to 2.75%. It would then be in slightly restrictive territory (we estimate
the upper bound of the neutral rate to be 2.5%). This rise would be
aimed primarily at anchoring inflation expectations and, consequently,
at curbing the pass-through effects of inflation. Monetary tightening
would therefore remain limited. Indeed, the overshoot of the inflation

target would be temporary, and the operational framework adopted by
the ECB in June 2025 allows it to tolerate this, provided it remains brief.
However, it cannot be ruled out that the ECB might take further action
if the decline in inflation proves slower than expected.
PUBLIC FINANCES ARE CONTINGENT ON RISING INTEREST RATES
The fiscal impulse in the Eurozone is expected to be clearly positive in
2026 due to the rise in defence and infrastructure spending in Germany.

This will contribute to a widening of the country’s primary deficit (from
+0.9 pp of GDP to 2.8%, according to our forecasts). The improvement in
public finances is expected to stall in both France and Italy, at least in
2026, due to the impact of inflation on the debt burden via index-linked
bonds. This trend is expected to reverse in 2027 with less expansionary
fiscal policies. Nevertheless, the increase in the debt service burden
would fuel a further rise in the public deficit, which would thus reach

around 3.3% and 3.5% of GDP in 2026 and 2027 respectively (compared
with 2.9% in 2025). Against this backdrop, the Eurozone’s public debt-
to-GDP ratio is projected to exceed 90% of GDP in 2026 and reach 93%
of GDP in 2027, according to our forecasts.
Article completed on 15 September 2026

## Document 245: FX_ The dollar story improves

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX_ The dollar story improves.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

We see limited scope for a near-term EUR/USD rebound. Key
factors for EUR/USD now are a hawkish Fed and a re-pricing of
the ECB cycle on French ﬁscal concerns, which have sent rate
diﬀerentials to their widest levels since 2022. Neither of those
factors appears set for an imminent reversal, leaving EUR/USD
to languish in a new 1.10-1.15 range
Chris Turner
EUR/USD looks set to stay pressured on wide rate diﬀerentials
Dollar enjoys some cyclical strength

We see EUR/USD remaining in a 1.10-1.15 range over the next three
to six months, as persistent US rate support and concerns over
French debt keep real rate diﬀerentials tilted against the euro.
It is hard to believe that EUR/USD traded above 1.20 in January.
Those fears of the dollar losing its safe-haven status as European
investors reacted to egregious US foreign policy have dissipated this
year as US cyclical strength has become more evident. There are no

signs of international investors leaving US markets, and by our

calculations, foreigners have kept their share of US bond and equity
markets steady at around 20%.
As we argued at the time, 'sell America’ moments have occurred in
the past and tend to be short-lived. Correlations now suggest the
dollar is playing its safe haven role again.
That dollar vulnerability at the start of the year was backed by a
weak US employment story and the prospect of further easing.
Today, the Federal Reserve is faced with a solid labour market,

above-target inﬂation and the prospect of taking policy into mildly
restrictive territory. While we are not looking for the central bank to
deliver on all three hikes priced in over the next 12 months, we
doubt that the current hawkish Fed pricing will leave the market
until next spring. At that point, base eﬀects look likely to bring
inﬂation much closer to the Fed's 2% target.
In contrast, the near 75bp of European Central Bank hikes priced
into the market looks more precarious. Heavy losses in French debt

dragged euro short-dated swap rates lower in early October, and
investors are rightfully questioning how the European Central Bank
plans to take policy into restrictive territory at the time of a looming
debt crisis.
That growing concern over French debt, which will probably be with
us right through to presidential elections next April, should keep real
rate diﬀerentials wide and keep EUR/USD under pressure. Higher oil
and gas prices aren't helping the euro either.

In all, we now see EUR/USD staying oﬀered in a 1.10-1.15 range
over the next three to six months, with downside risks should a
sharp deterioration in the French debt story demand a greater risk
premium for the euro.

### Page 3

What could hurt the dollar?
A key risk to our constructive dollar view is a renewed rise in FX
hedge ratios among foreign investors. While conﬁdence in the
dollar has pushed hedge ratios back to very low levels, any
renewed White House intervention in ﬁnancial, trade or foreign
policy could prompt investors to increase hedging and weigh on
the dollar.
A big topic for FX strategists over the last 18 months has been
foreign investor FX hedge ratios on US assets. Data suggests these

have returned to very low levels again as conﬁdence has returned
to the dollar story. As we witnessed brieﬂy in August, when the US
Treasury extended the duration of its Treasury buy-back operations,
the dollar was hit by White House intervention in ﬁnancial markets.
Any fresh White House intervention in ﬁnancial, trade or foreign
policy, perhaps after the November midterms, could see the dollar
come under pressure as investors raise hedge ratios again.

On the subject of the midterms, the base case of the Democrats
winning the House looks unlikely to move FX markets much. But a
Democratic clean sweep of Congress, and what it means for a
potential government shutdown, regulation, and constraints on the
AI build-out, could prove a cyclical negative for the dollar.

## Document 271: Lipper - This Week in Earnings 26Q3

Source PDF: all report pdf/Lipper - This Week in Earnings 26Q3.pdf
Report source: Refinitiv Lipper
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 24
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

PROPRIETARY RESEARCH FROM LSEG 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS

This Week in Earnings provides analysis and commentary on aggregate  
earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.

ANALYST: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA October 1, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com; 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 30.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.8%. 
 Of the 13 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 84.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 11.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 11.2%. 
 76.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 65 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 65 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 20.1. 
 During the week of Oct. 5, three S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $773.2B 
from $760.7B). Seven of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the materials (-9.1%) and health care (-2.2%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage decreases 
in earnings, while the energy (16.3%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. Overall, share-
weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 1.6% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the health care (-1.7B) and materials (-1.5B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level decreases in 
earnings, while the information technology (8.3B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 12.5B since the start of the quarter.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 30.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.8%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $773.2B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.9B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 504 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer Staples 
sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (114.7%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $55.1B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.7B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (445.6%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (116.1%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 25.1%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (66.5%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $256.1B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $153.8B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (135.6%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (68.9%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 25.3%. 
The Consumer Staples sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $33.7B 
in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $32.6B in 25Q3. 3 of the 10 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower 
earnings than a year ago. The Packaged Foods & Meats (-6.5%) and Household Products (-4.2%) sub-industries have 
the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 6.4%.

weighted earnings of $625.7B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 504 constituents) in 25Q4. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q4. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Real Estate sector 
has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q4. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (102.8%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $49.2B in 26Q4, 
compared to earnings of $24.3B in 25Q4. 3 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (230.6%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (129.3%) sub-industries have

The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (54.9%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $289.1B in 26Q4, compared to earnings of $186.6B in 25Q4. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (117%) and Semiconductor Materials &

the growth rate declines to 18.4%. 
The Real Estate sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (4.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $15.2B in 
26Q4, compared to earnings of $14.6B in 25Q4. 2 of the 13 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower 
earnings than a year ago. The Telecom Tower Reits (-13.3%) and Multi-Family Residential Reits (-0.1%) sub-industries 
have the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 7.1%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 31 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, three positive

surprises and five negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.

## Document 280: Next Generation EU_ The programme is coming to an end, but its effects on economic activity will endure (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Next Generation EU_ The programme is coming to an end, but its effects on economic activity will endure (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

23 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
NEXT GENERATION EU: THE PROGRAMME IS COMING TO AN END, BUT ITS EFFECTS ON ECONOMIC ACTIVITY WILL
ENDURE
Guillaume Derrien
A pillar of the NextGenerationEU programme, the Recovery and Resilience Facility (RRF) will expire on December 31, 2026. As of the
end of August 2026, slightly more than three-quarters of the total budget had been disbursed (EUR 440 billion out of EUR 573 billion).
Disbursing the remaining quarter (EUR 133 billion) by the end of the year will require a significant increase compared with the histo-

rical pace (EUR 90 billion per year). The goal of channeling these funds toward productive investment appears to have been achieved.
Tangible results for investment in Europe
According to Eurostat, public spending co-financed by the RRF totaled EUR 310 billion between 2020 and 2025. Nearly three-quarters (74%)
consisted of capital expenditures, including EUR 106 billion in direct public investment and EUR 122 billion in capital transfers to the private

sector (such as grants). This spending supported investment by businesses and households.
The list of major programmes funded by the RFF, published by the European Commission 1, shows that, within this scope, nearly two-thirds of
the funding went to transportation (primarily rail), energy infrastructure, and digital infrastructure (see Chart 1).
Another notable point: capital expenditures co-financed by the RRF increased significantly, reaching 0.4% of GDP for the European Union as a

whole in 2025 (see Chart 2). There are significant disparities among Member States, primarily due to differences in the amounts allocated to
them. Croatia (1.9%), Greece (1.2%), Italy (1%), and Spain (0.7%) received significant support, in contrast to France and Germany (0.1%). These
figures are consistent with the IMF’s estimate that the RRF added approximately 0.3 percentage points (pp) to EU growth in 2025 (more than
1 pp in Greece and Croatia, around 0.8 pp in Italy, and 0.5 pp in Spain) 2. Furthermore, as the first major joint European borrowing initiative, the

RRF made it possible to finance investment without increasing national budget deficits, giving several countries (Spain, Italy, Greece, Portugal)
valuable breathing room to consolidate their public finances.
1 Recovery and Resilience Scoreboard
2 The EU’s Recovery and Resilience Facility: Experience and Lessons for the Next Multiannual Financial Framework, WP/26/114, June 2026
SOURCE: EUROPEAN COMMISSION, Recovery and Resilience Scorecard

What impact on EU’s economic activity can we expect once this programme ends?
At first glance, effects will be limited. The end of the programme is expected to weigh on both public and private investment. However, the
strengthening of economic activity that we anticipate (see details in our latest Ecoperspectives – Q3 2026) rests primarily on German public

investment programmes and structural drivers (investment in AI, defence spending).
The ECB makes the same point in its September macroeconomic projections 3. It emphasises that the end of the RRF is expected to weigh
on public investment in 2027, while it anticipates an acceleration in business investment. Furthermore, RRF funds are being absorbed on
the ground only gradually. Since most disbursements are concentrated toward the end of the programme, the effects of the RRF will extend

beyond 2026.
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

## Document 282: Nuclear energy_ One industry, many reactors Our latest primer explores the reactor technologies and hurdles shaping nuclear’s next chapter. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Nuclear energy_ One industry, many reactors Our latest primer explores the reactor technologies and hurdles shaping nuclear’s next chapter. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-09-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13022014     1

Sustainability 
Nuclear energy: One industry, many reactors 
29 September 2026

Key takeaways 
• As power demand grows, nuclear has returned to the forefront of the energy conversation. Yet the industry is far from one-size-
fits-all, encompassing a range of reactor technologies designed to serve different markets, applications and deployment models. 
• Nuclear power is evolving beyond traditional large-scale reactors. While proven designs continue to anchor utility-scale 
generation, small modular reactors (SMRs) and microreactors are being developed to serve a broader range of applications 
through more flexible, modular deployment models, though most remain in the demonstration phase today. 
• The future of nuclear deployment will depend on more than reactor design alone. Supply chains, skilled labor, fuel availability and 
regulatory approvals remain critical challenges, though recent policy initiatives are intended to support licensing, fuel production 
and broader industry growth.

Nuclear energy has re-emerged at the center of the power conversation, driven by rising electricity demand and tightening 
supply-demand dynamics across the grid. Yet nuclear is not a single technology. It encompasses a wide range of reactor designs 
and sizes, each with distinct economics, risks and deployment pathways. Building off our previous nuclear publication (which can 
be found here: Nuclear energy: Who, what, where, when, why?), this primer unpacks that complexity, grounding the broader 
nuclear narrative in the practical realities of development, from technology and construction to fuel, licensing and execution.  
Reactor sizes 
Nuclear power is best understood as a collection of reactor classes differentiated by scale, deployment model and end use 
(Exhibit 1). Rather than competing directly with one another, these technologies are designed to serve different segments of the 
power market and, in many cases, can scale alongside one another. 
Exhibit 1: Different reactor types have different advantages and risks 
Comparison of reactor technologies

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
I NSTI TUTE
Accessible version

### Page 2

2 29 September 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Key Distinctions  
• Large reactors (≥1 gigawatt (GW)): Designed for large-scale electricity generation, these reactors provide consistent, high-
capacity power and serve as core assets within the grid. 
• Small modular reactors (SMRs)(~50–300 megawatts (MW)): Mid-scale systems designed for modular deployment across 
utilities, industrial users and large commercial customers, offering greater flexibility in siting and capacity expansion.  
• Microreactors (≤20 MW): Small, highly distributed systems intended for niche or off-grid applications where reliability, 
autonomy or energy security are primary considerations. 
Technology 
Nuclear power generation is based on a common principle: heat produced from nuclear fission is used to generate steam, which 
drives a turbine to create electricity (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: In a nuclear reactor, heat is formed by fission 
Process for a nuclear fission reaction

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Nuclear reactor designs can be grouped based on coolant type, neutron spectrum and fuel configuration. Most reactors currently 
operating in the US are light-water reactors (LWRs), though a wide range of advanced designs are being developed (Exhibit 3).  
Exhibit 3: Gen III & Gen IV+ technologies have different characteristics 
Reactor technologies 
Reactor Type Coolant Moderator Neutron Spectrum Operating Temperature Pressure 
Pressurized Water Reactor (PWR) Water Light water Thermal ~300–330°C High (~150 bar) 
Boiling Water Reactor (BWR) Water Light water Thermal ~280–290°C High (lower than PWR) 
Heavy Water Reactor (HWR) Heavy water Heavy water Thermal ~300°C High 
Molten Salt Reactor (MSR) Molten salt Graphite (typical) Thermal / fast (design-dependent) ~600–750°C Low 
High-Temperature Gas Reactor (HTGR) Helium gas Graphite Thermal ~700–800°C Low 
Sodium Fast Reactor (SFR) Liquid sodium None Fast ~450–550°C Low 
Gas-Cooled Fast Reactor (GFR) Helium gas None Fast ~700–850°C Low 
Source: BofA Global Research, Energy Information Administration (EIA), Idaho National Laboratory (INL), Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
AP1000 
The AP1000 is a Generation III+ pressurized water reactor with a capacity of approximately 1.1 GW per unit (Exhibit 4). Its design 
is centered around passive safety systems that rely on gravity, natural circulation and compressed gas, rather than large numbers 
of active pumps and diesel-backed safety systems. By reducing the number of safety-critical moving parts, this approach lowers 
system complexity and supports a more standardized large-reactor platform.

### Page 3

29 September 2026    3

I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 4: AP1000s typically need about 1,000 acres of land 
Diagram of an AP1000 nuclear plant

Source: BofA Global Research, Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
The AP1000’s design emphasizes open-top construction, with large structural and equipment modules manufactured offsite and 
then assembled at the project site. This approach is intended to reduce onsite labor needs, improve quality control through 
factory fabrication and support a more repeatable, standardized construction process. 
The AP1000 is no longer a conceptual technology. It is a licensed, utility-scale nuclear design already operating in the field. As a 
result, the key remaining considerations center on construction costs, project execution and the economics of deploying 
additional units, according to BofA Global Research. 
SMRs 
Small modular reactors (SMRs) are nuclear power plants generally 300 MWe or less per unit and are designed around a factory-
based, modular construction model (Exhibit 5). Unlike traditional gigawatt-scale nuclear projects, SMRs represent a shift toward 
repeatable, standardized deployment (read our prior SMR publication: Q&A quick guide: Small modular reactors). Major 
components can be manufactured in a factory setting and then transported to project sites for assembly, reducing the amount of 
onsite construction required. Their standardized designs also create opportunities for learning-by-doing, potentially lowering 
costs over time. In addition, capacity can be added incrementally through discrete modules, allowing capital to be deployed in 
stages rather than committed upfront.

### Page 4

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Market landscape  
There are dozens of SMR designs globally, with more than 100 projects at various stages of development, though only a limited 
number are currently operational or under construction. While developers are pursuing a range of reactor technologies, light-
water reactor (LWR) designs still dominate the US development landscape (Exhibit 6). According to BofA Global Research, the 
sector remains largely in a development and demonstration phase rather than broad commercial deployment. 
Exhibit 6: LWRs make up 50% of the SMR designs 
US SMR designs by technology type (%)

Source: Energy Information Administration (EIA), BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

50%
SFR

### Page 5

29 September 2026    5

I NSTI TUTE

Microreactors 
Microreactors are nuclear power systems typically 20 MWe or less per unit, representing the smallest class of advanced reactors 
(Exhibit 7). They emphasize flexibility, allowing deployment where traditional grid-scale infrastructure is impractical. Many 
designs incorporate multi-year fuel cycles that reduce refueling requirements and associated logistics burdens, making them 
particularly attractive for remote communities, industrial sites and defense-related applications. 
Exhibit 7: Microreactors need about one to five acres of land depending on the design 
Diagram of a microreactor

Source: BofA Global Research, Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Market landscape  
A growing number of developers are pursuing microreactor technologies in the US, however, despite increasing interest, most 
projects remain in the demonstration and pilot phase rather than commercial deployment. According to BofA Global Research, 
federal government programs continue to play a central role in funding and advancing early deployments, with several initial 
projects targeted for later this decade. 
Supply chain 
Large reactors depend on specialized components and complex construction processes, limiting the number of qualified 
suppliers. Modular designs, on the other hand, shift a greater share of manufacturing from construction sites to factory 
environments, with the goal of improving consistency, reducing onsite complexity and enabling more standardized deployment. 
As reactor sizes decrease, component requirements generally become less specialized, expanding the potential supplier base and 
simplifying project execution. 
Workforce availability and quality assurance remain critical across all reactor types. Large projects require significant numbers of 
skilled tradespeople, including welders, electricians and nuclear-certified inspectors. The US nuclear workforce has declined over 
prior decades, leading to labor shortages during first-of-a-kind builds.  
Fuel 
Fuel is one of the most important building blocks in the advanced nuclear ecosystem, as availability, manufacturing capacity and 
regulatory approval are all critical to deployment. Because many next-generation reactors require specialized fuels, the

### Page 6

6 29 September 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

development of commercial-scale fuel supply chains will be a key factor in determining how quickly advanced nuclear 
technologies can be brought to market. 
HALEU 
High-assay low-enriched uranium (HALEU) refers to uranium enriched above the roughly 5% U-235 content used in conventional 
reactors but below the 20% threshold for highly-enriched uranium. HALEU enables smaller reactor cores and longer fuel cycles, 
making it a key requirement for many advanced reactor designs. 
Historically, commercial HALEU supply has been limited. To address this gap, the Department of Energy launched the HALEU 
Availability Program in 2024 to support enrichment, fuel processing and deconversion capacity. The government is also working 
to recover HALEU from existing materials, with a targeted recovery of roughly 10 to 26 metric tons over time. 
The first US HALEU production in decades occurred in 2023, though output remains limited. Current production is approximately 
900 kilograms annually and is government-owned, with material allocated primarily to research, fuel qualification and early 
demonstration projects. 
TRISO 
Tri-structural isotropic particle fuel (TRISO) is key for high-temperature gas-cooled reactors (HTGRs) and certain microreactors. 
Unlike conventional nuclear fuel, it requires specialized manufacturing and therefore is currently only limited to pilot-scale 
production. But key development efforts include DOE-led research through the Advanced Gas Reactor (AGR) program and 
expansion of private-sector manufacturing with DOE support. 
Regulatory approval is also required for new fuel designs, manufacturing processes and transportation systems. As a result, 
scaling TRISO production remains an important step toward the broader deployment of advanced reactors. 
Fuel Risk 
Fuel availability is a key execution risk for advanced nuclear projects. Many next-generation reactor designs rely on HALEU or 
TRISO fuel, both of which remain supply-constrained. As a result, reactor technology may be ready before fuel supply is, creating 
a potential bottleneck for deployment.  
Challenges include multi-year lead times for fuel production and certification, potential gaps between reactor licensing and fuel 
readiness, and the limited supplier base and transportation requirements associated with HALEU. Together, these factors can 
add uncertainty to project timelines and costs until fuel supply chains reach commercial scale. 
Licensing 
Licensing is a key determinant of nuclear project timelines, capital deployment and execution risk. Regulatory requirements 
shape not only whether a project is approved, but also how predictable construction schedules are and how easily reactor 
designs can be replicated. In the US, commercial reactors are licensed by the Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC), whose 
framework emphasizes safety reviews, environmental assessments and public participation. While this approach provides 
regulatory certainty, it can also create lengthy approval timelines. 
Large light-water reactors benefit from well-established regulatory processes, while frameworks for advanced reactors continue 
to evolve. For first-of-a-kind projects, licensing can extend four to eight years or longer depending on technology maturity, 
application quality, public intervention and regulatory resources.  
Policy  
Recent years have seen a flurry of initiatives to revitalize nuclear energy. A series of coordinated policy moves, demonstration 
programs, fuel supply efforts and industry partnerships have converged to create the conditions for a potential nuclear 
resurgence:  
• ADVANCE Act: Signed into law in July 2024, the ADVANCE (Accelerating Deployment of Versatile, Advanced Nuclear for 
Clean Energy) Act modernizes the NRC licensing process (making it more affordable and predictable). The legislation also  
facilitates the siting of new reactors at brownfield sites, supports domestic fuel-cycle capabilities, and includes broader NRC 
institutional reforms, such as workforce recruitment and retention initiatives, updates to the agency’s mission statement and 
provisions related to international nuclear cooperation and exports. 
• Executive orders: On May 23, 2025, the Trump administration issued four executive orders aimed at accelerating US nuclear 
deployment through licensing reform, expedited reactor testing, expanded national security applications and efforts to 
strengthen the domestic nuclear fuel and supply chain base. Since their issuance, federal agencies, particularly the 
Department of Energy, have taken steps to implement these directives. While progress has been made on several fronts, a 
number of the orders' longer-term objectives remain under active implementation, according to BofA Global Public Policy.

### Page 7

7 29 September 2026

I NSTI TUTE

## Document 304: Supply chain risks are changing. Shouldn’t financing change too_

### Page 1

As climate risks have a larger impact on supply chains, the
need to ﬁnance resilience-building initiatives is increasing.
Banks have an important role to play by expanding their
ﬁnancing toolkit, while companies with proactive resilience
strategies can gain greater access to capital
Coco Zhang
As climate risks become supply chain risks, ﬁnancing supply chain resilience is
oﬀering opportunities for banks and companies to explore together
Climate and weather-related shocks are a growing source of supply

chain risk. In August, the Rhine River’s water levels hit a record low
since 1880, hindering trade ﬂows across Europe. In 2024, Hurricane
Helene aﬀected global tech and medical supply chains by ﬂooding
key ultra-pure quartz production facilities in the US. The upcoming
“very strong” El Niño could substantially disrupt temperature and
precipitation patterns across the globe next year, putting, for
example, food production and trade at risk.
These events underscore the need to build supply chain resilience.

Extreme weather is now a more impactful part of the supply chain

risk landscape alongside geopolitics and other risks.
Yet signiﬁcant gaps remain. First, while the awareness of supply
chain climate risks is rising, many companies have yet to
acknowledge their ﬁnancial materiality. Second, the uptake of
ﬁnancing solutions has not kept pace with supply chain resilience
needs, despite trade ﬁnance supporting around 90% of global
trade. This leaves ﬁnancial institutions well positioned to expand
ﬁnancing solutions that support corporate supply chain resilience.

Below is how they can do it.
Expanding banks’ toolkit for supply chain
resilience
1. Broaden the use of sustainable ﬁnance
Today, the most common way banks support supply chain
resilience is through sustainable ﬁnance – in other words,
sustainability-labelled debt. However, scaling up this market
remains challenging because supply chain resilience is not a
standalone category under widely used frameworks such as the
International Capital Market Association’s (ICMA’s) Green Bond

Principles and Green Loan Principles. This makes it harder for issuers
to ﬁnance projects under a dedicated supply chain resilience
theme. As a result, there is also limited data on the total volume of
sustainable ﬁnance supporting supply chain resilience. The global
green bond use-of-proceeds allocation data, for example,
demonstrates this challenge.

But at the same time, this creates an opportunity. Supply chains
are closely linked to many existing sustainable ﬁnance themes.
Projects that strengthen supply chain resilience can often be
ﬁnanced under established categories such as climate adaptation
(e.g. resilient infrastructure and information systems), sustainable
water management (e.g. ﬂood mitigation), circular economy, and
so on. This requires banks to better understand which supply chain

challenges matter most to each client and identify the
sustainability solutions best suited to their needs.
2. Look beyond labelled sustainable debt
The opportunity to ﬁnance supply chain resilience extends well
beyond traditional sustainable debt markets. Here are some
examples:
Supply chain resilience ﬁnance
The traditional supply chain and trade ﬁnance solutions
can be tailored to integrate resilience objectives. For
example, guarantees can support cross-border trade in

climate-resilient goods, while preferential ﬁnancing terms
can reward companies that actively manage supply chain
risks. By incorporating supply chain risk assessments into
ﬁnancing programmes and linking working capital
solutions to supplier resilience, these products can help
companies better withstand disruptions and support long-
term growth.
Climate and resilience tech ﬁnance

### Page 4

Banks can provide ﬁnancing and advisory services to help
scale and deploy technologies that strengthen supply
chain resilience. Examples include water management
technologies, regenerative agriculture, and indoor farming.
These solutions can improve the upstream supply chains
of industries such as food, beverage, and hospitality. As
interest in resilience technologies grows, ﬁnancial
institutions are increasingly developing tailored ﬁnancing
solutions to support their adoption.

Blended ﬁnance
Some resilience technologies, projects, and markets may
be too risky for commercial banks to ﬁnance on their own.
Blended ﬁnance oﬀers a compelling solution, as it
combines private capital with support from multilateral
development banks or other public institutions. This can
reduce risk for lenders while helping companies access
more aﬀordable funding for resilience-related investments.
For banks, these solutions reﬂect a shift in mindset and strategy.

Moving beyond ﬁnancing individual transactions, banks now play
an important role in helping build a resilient ecosystem of suppliers,
infrastructure, and logistics networks.

As banks work to expand their ﬁnancing oﬀerings to enhance
supply chain resilience, companies would beneﬁt from doing their

part as well. My colleague Rico Luman has written about some best
practices companies can adopt in their day-to-day operations.
These include building up inventory of high-risk supply materials,
diversifying suppliers and transport routes, and improving supply
chain visibility. Financing solutions that link resilience to working
capital, for example, can help companies strengthen these eﬀorts.
Companies can also look beyond day-to-day operations and invest

in longer-term resilience initiatives, such as climate-resilient
materials, water management technologies, and other adaptation
solutions. These investments can help create more resilient supply
chains while opening up additional ﬁnancing opportunities.
Treat climate risks as business risks – and
ﬁnancing opportunities
As climate risks become supply chain risks, resilience needs
ﬁnancing. The available toolkit for ﬁnancing supply chain resilience

oﬀers more potential than what the market is consistently tapping
today. This creates considerable opportunities for banks and
companies to explore together.

～政権交代観測と RBNZ のマンデート変更が NZ ドルの重しに～

（要旨） 
○ ニュージーランドでは 10 月１日に議会が解散し、11 月７日の総選挙に向けた選挙戦が本格
化した。 二大政党の支持率低下が続くなか、 土地税の導入と年間1.94 万ＮＺドルのベーシッ
クインカムに相当する 「市民所得」 の支給を掲げる新興のオポチュニティ党が支持を伸ばし、
初の議席獲得と連立政権の行方を左右する可能性が浮上している。 世論調査では、 最大野党 ・
労働党を中心とする左派陣営が同党の支持を得れば政権を樹立し得る一方、現与党連合が過
半数を確保できるかも不透明である。 
○ 金融市場では、政権交代時にRBNZ（ニュージーランド準備銀行）の政策目標へ「持続可能な
雇用の最大化」 が再び加えられる可能性が意識され、 ＮＺドルは対米ドルで10 ヵ月ぶりの安
値圏に弱含んでいる。足元の失業率が10 年ぶりの高水準となるなか、 マンデート変更が実現
すれば追加利上げのハードルが高まるとの見方もあり、総選挙後の連立協議と金融政策への
影響がＮＺドル相場の先行きを左右する可能性がある。

ニュージーランド議会（代議院：一院制（総議席数120） ）が10 月１日に解散し、11 月７日に実
施予定の総選挙に向けた動きが正式に始まった。 同国では、30 年前にドイツ型の小選挙区比例代表
併用制（ＭＭＰ）が導入されて以来、右派の国民党、ないし左派の労働党を中心とする連立政権が
一般的となっている。さらに、このところは二大政党の支持率の低下傾向に歯止めがかからず、多
数政党との連立による政権樹立を余儀なくされる展開が続いている。 
こうしたなか、今回の総選挙では、新興政党・オポチュニティ党が「台風の目」となる可能性が
高まっている。同党は結党から 10 年を迎えるものの、過去の総選挙では議席獲得に必要な得票率
基準（５％）を満たすことができなかった。しかし、最新の世論調査では、支持率が基準を上回っ
て初めて議席を獲得し、キャスティングボートを握る可能性が高まっている。背景には、同党が掲
げる公約が影響している。同党は、都市部で年間1.75％、農村部で0.5％の土地税を新設し、それ
を財源の一部として、 原則18 歳以上の国民などに年間 1.94 万ＮＺドルのベーシックインカム （Ｂ
Ｉ）に相当する「市民所得」を支給する税制改正を公約に掲げる。同党はその目的について、国民
生活の下支えに加え、 高止まりする住宅価格の抑制につながるとの見方を示す（図１） 。さらに、 同

## Document 432: 三季度全球智能手机出货量暴跌22%，创有纪录以来最大降幅

Source PDF: all report pdf/三季度全球智能手机出货量暴跌22%，创有纪录以来最大降幅.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 3

### Page 1

暴跌的 “ 元凶 ” 是存储芯⽚成本狂飙 350% ，终端被迫涨价致消费者换机意愿降⾄冰点。全球
前五⼤品牌出货量全线溃退，国内市场更创 2013 年来最低，头部仅华为与苹果逆势增⻓。
机构悲观预计，市场尚未触底，⾏业寒冬的复苏⾄少被推迟⾄ 2028 年。
摘要：
存储芯⽚危机持续冲击全球智能⼿机市场。
当地时间 10 ⽉ 15 ⽇，国际数据公司（ IDC ）发布《全球季度⼿机追踪报告》初步数据称， 2026
年第三季度全球智能⼿机出货量降⾄ 2.553 亿部，同⽐萎缩 22.0% ，创下有纪录以来季度同⽐最
⼤降幅。
IDC 研究⼈员表⽰，各项指标表明，市场尚未触底。存储芯⽚成本较去年上涨了约 350% ，⽽且
仍在继续攀升，给⼚商的利润率和出货量带来巨⼤压⼒。尽管存储芯⽚价格涨势开始放缓 ——
第三季度环⽐涨幅降⾄较低的两位数百分⽐，此前⼏个季度的环⽐涨幅约为 50% ⾄ 100%—— 但
其他关键零部件的成本依然居⾼不下，进⼀步加剧了⾏业困境。
随着上游价格传导⾄零售终端，许多消费者选择延⻓现有设备的使⽤时间，导致⼿机销量下滑

明显。 IDC 数据显⽰，今年三季度，在全球智能⼿机出货量中，前五⼤品牌出货量同⽐均出现下
滑， OPPO 、 vivo 同⽐降幅均超过 30% 。
从市场份额来看，今年三季度，在全球智能⼿机出货量中，三星、苹果、⼩⽶、 OPPO 、 vivo 排
名前五位。其中，三星、苹果的市场份额相较去年继续增⻓，分别达到 22.6% 和 22.4% ；⼩⽶、
OPPO 、 vivo 的份额分别为 12% 、 10% 和 7.5% ，同⽐略显下滑。
来源： IDC 官⽹
IDC 预计，市场增⻓将持续受限⾄ 2027 年，复苏⾄少要推迟到 2028 年。报告表⽰，越来越多的
原始设备制造商意识到，⾏业成本结构正⾯临永久性结构性转变。
IDC 研究⼈员称，尽管销售疲软，但⼀些供应商在明年零部件供应预期收紧之前提⾼了⽣产计
划。如果需求仍然疲软，这可能会导致 2027 年的库存增加，带来进⼀步的下⾏⻛险。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
今年第三季度，中国市场的智能⼿机出货量也呈现⼤幅下降。
近⽇，数据机构 Omdia 发布的 2026 年第三季度中国⼤陆智能⼿机统计数据显⽰，本季度国内智
能⼿机总出货量 5930 万台，同⽐下滑 12% ，创下⾃ 2013 年以来同期最低出货⽔平。市场格局出
现明显分化，前五品牌中只有华为与苹果实现正增⻓。
IDC 发布的中国智能⼿机出货量数据报告则显⽰，第三季度国内智能⼿机市场出货量约为 5790
万台，同⽐下降 15.4% 。华为是头部⼚商中唯⼀实现同⽐双位数增⻓的品牌。
来源： IDC 中国官⽹
IDC 表⽰，相较上半年约 4% 的同⽐降幅，下半年中国整体市场规模下滑速度进⼀步加快。存储
及其他元器件成本⼤幅上升所引发的终端价格上涨与产品配置⽅案调整，已开始明显抑制消费
需求，其中安卓⼚商与⻓尾品牌承受的压⼒尤为突出。

在 IDC 看来，第三季度中国市场呈现 “ 量跌价升 ” 的典型特征。上游成本压⼒持续向终端传导，导
致新品定价普遍上浮，同时⼚商不得不调整产品配置组合以平衡成本。消费者⾯对价格上涨仍
需时间适应，换机周期进⼀步拉⻓，直接拖累整体出货表现。这⼀趋势表明，成本压⼒已成为
当前影响中国智能⼿机市场⾛势的关键变量。
据该机构预计，存储及其他物料成本将在未来⼏个季度依然处于⾼位，⼿机产品价格将继续保
持增⻓趋势。⼤部分消费者对价格上涨仍需接受与适应的时间，换机周期恐进⼀步拉⻓。因
此，即便有⼤量各档位新品集中发布，预计 2026 年第四季度中国智能⼿机市场表现依然承压。
甚⾄到 2027 年⼤部分时间，成本压⼒仍将存在，市场预计仍将延续明显下滑趋势。
Omdia 则预测， 2026 全年，中国⼤陆智能⼿机出货量同⽐将下滑约 7% 。该机构表⽰，在存储成
本⾼企的⼤环境下，⼚商需要平衡研发投⼊、硬件成本与终端定价，只有把 AI 能⼒转化为可落地
的⾼频实⽤场景，才能够真正撬动存量市场的换机需求。
本⽂来源：界⾯新闻

環保 
可寧衛 (8422 TT)

重新覆蓋  
買進(Buy) 
既有處理業務穩固，新廠與再利用事業接續貢獻  
⚫ 營收規模擴大，後續獲利動能逐步增強  
⚫ 整治專案支撐營運，再生粒 料與綠電增添成長動能  
⚫ 與台灣人壽合作的南區新焚化廠，預計 2029 年起成為長期動能  
營收規模擴大，後續獲利動能逐步增強 ：可寧衛 1H26 合併營收 33.82 
億元，毛利率 37% 、營益率 27% ，EPS 0.55 元。南區  BOT 建造收入
自 5 月起依工程進度認列，上半年約  10.3 億元，帶動合併營收擴大；
7、8 月分別認列 0.8 億元及  2.6 億元，後續金額將隨施工進度變動。
展望下半年， 205 兵工廠整治案認列可望高於上半年，南區焚化舊廠
的處理量與發電效率持續改善，虧損可望收斂。明年則隨大承再生粒
料業務逐步投入營運，為營收及獲利增添新動能 。 
整治專案支撐營運，再生粒料與綠電增添成長動能 ：205 兵工廠既有
合約 24 億元，因實際挖出的有害廢棄物比率高於原預期，固化與掩
埋需求增加；既有工作預計於 1H27 完成，預期另有數億元潛在追加
預算。新業務方面，大承再生粒料廠規劃月處理量約  9,000 噸，待試
運轉及審查完成後，可望成為 後續的成長動能 ；其產品可供吉鼎製成
道路回填用低強度混凝土，而吉鼎現有一般混凝土業務已開始獲利。
此外，漁電共生一期 69MW 已併聯，二期規劃約 70MW ，目前仍在申
設。  
與台灣人壽合作的南區新焚化廠，預計 2029 年起 成為 長期動能 ：可
寧衛與台壽共同投資吉信，持股分別為  55% 及 45%；BOT 案包含約  
3.5 年興建期及後續  20 年營運期。興建期間同步操作的舊廠，經設
備調整後每日處理量已回升至 1,000~1,200 噸、每噸發電效率恢復至
約 300 度， 2H26 舊廠 虧損可望收斂。新廠規劃每日處理量  1,350 
噸、年發電量約  3.5 億度，預期於 2029 年開始貢獻處理費與售電收
入；穩定營運後年營收規模約 15~18 億元。我們預估 可寧衛 2026/2027
年 EPS  1.31 /1.67 元 ， 給予「買進 」評等， 目標價 31.8 元(2027 年 EPSx 19
倍)。 
風險因子： 專案認列與追加預算不如預期、投產及工程遞延、設備運
轉異常 。

2 
 可寧衛以事業廢棄物處理起家，業務涵蓋有害廢棄物固化、最終掩埋及污染場址整治，並逐步拓展至再生粒料、混凝土、焚化廠營運及再生能源。公司
於高雄岡山的兩座掩埋場持續營運，分別服務有害及一般事業廢棄物。掩埋
場許可與可用容 量 不 易 取 得 ， 集 團 提 供 從 開 挖 、 檢 測 、 中 間 處 理 到 最 終 處 置
的服務。  
圖表一、 可寧衛集團 事業群

資料來源： 可寧衛 ，中信投顧整理  
205 案 包 括 污 染 土 壤 開 挖 、 分 類 檢 測 及 後 續 處 理 。 現 場 有 害 物 質 比 率 較 原 估
高，帶動固化與掩埋需求，也使既有預算消耗較快。工程按實際施作進度認
列，雨天、現場檢測及業主預算程序均會影響單季金額。 預 期 將 有 數億元潛
在追加預算 ，可提高剩餘工期的收入貢獻。  
南區 BOT 案於新廠興建期間同步操作舊廠。舊廠設備使用年限長，接手後需
逐步檢修與調整 ； 近期每日處理量回升至 1,000 ~1,200 噸 ， 發電效率亦有改善。
處理量提升可增加處理費收入，發電效率改善則有助於售電及降低外購電需
求。 可寧衛與台灣人壽共同投資吉信，持股分別為 55% 及 45% ，承作南區焚
化廠 BOT 案。合約期間共 23.5 年，包含 3.5 年興建期及 20 年操作期，總投
資金額約 150 億元。新廠規劃三爐兩機，每日處理量 1,350 噸、年發電量約
3.5 億度；完工後將透過廢棄物處理費與售電提供長期營運貢獻，穩定運作後
年營收規模估約 15～18 億元。

大承再生粒料廠透過固化及乾燥製程處理污泥、底渣等廢棄物， 設 計 處 理 量
約每月 9,000 噸 。 產 出 的 再 生 粒 料 可 供 吉 鼎 製 成 控 制 性 低 強 度 回 填 材 料
(CLSM )，應用於道路等 工程。大承目前仍在試 運轉與審查階段，待正 式投入
營運、收料量逐步提高後， 有望 成為 後續 的 獲 利 成 長 動 能。 吉 鼎 現 有 一 般 混
凝土業務已開始貢獻營收與獲利，後續 CLSM 業務則仰賴大承穩定供料及客
戶導入。月設計處理量 9,000 噸， 預估 大承滿載年化處理收入約 4~5.5 億元。

## Document 512: 央行规范银行间债券市场债券发行行为，禁止发行人认购、变相认购自己发行的债券

Source PDF: all report pdf/央行规范银行间债券市场债券发行行为，禁止发行人认购、变相认购自己发行的债券.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
《规则》禁⽌发⾏⼈认购、变相认购⾃⼰发⾏的债券，并规定任何⼈不得直接或间接协助
发⾏⼈认购或变相认购⾃⼰发⾏的债券；禁⽌发⾏⼈⼲预投资者认购决策或以返费等⽅式
⼲扰定价；明确不同债券发⾏⽅式的定价要求；推动发⾏⼈优化承销机构选聘机制和选聘
标准，提⾼债券承销服务质量。
摘要：
为规范银⾏间债券市场债券发⾏⾏为，维护市场秩序，促进债券市场⾼质量发展，中国⼈⺠银
⾏制定了《银⾏间债券市场债券发⾏管理规则》（下称《规则》）， 10 ⽉ 16 ⽇⾯向社会公开征求意
⻅。
《规则》适⽤于境内及境外发⾏⼈在银⾏间债券市场发⾏的，约定按期还本付息的有价证券。
债券定价是债券发⾏的核⼼环节，关系到债券市场能否将⾦融资源精准配置到实体经济，也是
实现债券市场⾼质量发展的重要前提。
《规则》明确，债券发⾏应当遵循市场化、法治化、公平公正、诚实信⽤原则。债券的发⾏价
格、利率及发⾏过程中涉及的佣⾦费⽤等应当以市场化⽅式确定。任何单位、个⼈不得影响债
券公允定价，不得有违反公平竞争、  进⾏利益输送、  谋取不正当利益、  损害投资者权益、破坏

市场秩序的⾏为。
《规则》共⼆⼗⼋条，从发⾏⼈、承销商、投资⼈三个维度强化对债券发⾏⾏为的规范。
⼀是规范发⾏⼈的发⾏定价⾏为。具体来说，《规则》禁⽌发⾏⼈认购、变相认购⾃⼰发⾏的债
券，并规定任何⼈不得直接或间接协助发⾏⼈认购或变相认购⾃⼰发⾏的债券；禁⽌发⾏⼈⼲
预投资者认购决策或以返费等⽅式⼲扰定价；明确不同债券发⾏⽅式的定价要求；推动发⾏⼈
优化承销机构选聘机制和选聘标准，提⾼债券承销服务质量。
⼆是规范承销机构的承销⾏为。《规则》禁⽌低价承销，规定承销费不得明显低于⾏业公允⽔
平；禁⽌事先承诺债券投标价格或发⾏价格（利率）  ；规范包销、代投代缴等⾏为；强化承销
机构利益冲突、信息隔离等内控要求，加强信息披露等市场化约束机制等。
三是规范投资者的认购⾏为。《规则》禁⽌投资者虚假投标；不得蓄意⼤量申报并撤销，扰乱债
券发⾏价格（利率）及配售；不得以⼲扰价格为⽬的，投标价格明显低于市场公允价格。  《规
则》对内幕交易、利益输送等⾏为也提出明确禁⽌性要求。
四是规范簿记建档等发⾏流程。《规则》要求簿记场所应当能够全程记录所有询价、调整、定价

决策信息记录，并形成不可篡改的记录，供事后核查。
五是强化对发⾏定价环节的监督管理。  《规则》明确中国⼈⺠银⾏对银⾏间债券市场债券发⾏
进⾏监督管理；建⽴⼀线监测、⾃律管理、⾏政监管和统⼀执法闭环管理机制；明确中国⼈⺠
银⾏及其分⽀机构可以采取责令整改、监管谈话等监管措施，并依法对违法违规⾏为予以⾏政
处罚；加强⾏刑衔接，  对于涉嫌犯罪的⾏为，  移送司法机关依法追究刑事责任。
来源：澎湃新闻
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

### Page 1

本周 AI 投资回报前景遭市场质疑，费城半导体指数累跌 11.4% ，纳指单周跌超 4% 。
CoreWeave 股价周累跌逾 25% ，债券跌⾄困境⽔平，⾼息债务融资模式受到质疑。策略师
警⽰过度看空存在踏空⻛险， " ⽊头姐 " 则逆势加仓 CoreWeave 。
摘要：
⼀场围绕⼈⼯智能投资回报前景的市场质疑将芯⽚股推⼊⼋连跌困境，创下 2008 年⾦融危机以
来最⻓连跌纪录。
本周纳指累跌超 4% 、创 2025 年 4 ⽉关税动荡以来最差单周表现。费城半导体指数本周累跌
11.4% ，过去⼋个交易⽇累计下跌逾 13% 、蒸发市值约 2 万亿美元。
AI 基础设施建设成本⾼企、⽽企业盈利回报前景模糊，正令投资者重新审视这⼀赛道的逻辑根
基。嘉盛分析师 Fawad Razaqzada 表⽰：
美国银⾏的 Michael Hartnett 警告称，未来数周市场将以规避⻛险为主基调，投资者正寻求回避
AI 交易、⾼利率环境及美国中期选举带来的不确定性。
芯⽚股连跌纪录， 2 万亿美元市值蒸发

费城半导体指数本周累跌 11.4% ，连续下跌⼋个交易⽇，创 2008 年⾦融危机以来最⻓连跌纪
录，这也是该指数今年内第五次出现⾄少 10% 的回调。
据 Stocktwits ，半导体股票市值过去⼋连跌已蒸发约 2 万亿美元。若以各股较⾃⾝ 52 周⾼点的跌
幅计算，整个半导体板块损失市值约达 4 万亿美元。尽管如此，费城半导体指数年内⾄今仍累涨
约 60% 。
周五追踪该指数的 ETF （ SOXQ ）⼤幅下挫近 4% ， Stocktwits 平台上散⼾对 SOXQ 的情绪在过去
⼀天内由 " 中性 " 降⾄ " 看跌 " 。
本周的价格⾛势表明，市场辩论的焦点开始转移。问题不再是 AI 的市场空间有多⼤，⽽是实现这
⼀⽬标所需的巨额投⼊最终能有多少盈利。

预⽰财务困境的⽔平，折射出市场对⾼收益债务融资模式的深层担忧。

本持续攀升。

持了 CoreWeave 及 Cerebras 。

施的融资成本，这对 AI 交易构成双重不利。
这⼀切都会过去，原因很简单，它将是变⾰性的，将以积极的⽅式影响每⼀个⾏业和每⼀个国

 上週美股市場概況：
上週美股在經濟數據將降溫中稱底下展現出顯著韌性，但盤面仍分化與估值壓力同步加劇。最新公布
的 9 月新增非農就業大幅走降至 2.9 萬人，同時失業率升至 4.2%，加上 8 月核心 PCE 溫和放緩與聯
準會官員偏鴿發言，使 10 月暫停加息的市場預期急升；然而，10 年期美債殖利率一度飆破 5.3% 創
年內高點、30 年期觸及 5.62% 的二十多年新高，疊加美伊局勢擾動油價與避險資金回流股市，持續
壓制大盤折現估值並使多數產業收跌，全週盤面全靠資訊科技上漲 1.4% 與費半 3.7% 在半導體、軟
體等相關科技類股帶動下勉強支撐，掩蓋了醫療、金融等八大板塊的廣泛疲弱，其中東芝擴產重創威

騰與希捷等 HDD 概念股逾 10%，拖累記憶體相關族群，以及零售端價格戰浮現，充分反映出當下
因基本面及利率壓力下的複雜度 ，市場廣度仍在低位 ，因此三大指數方面僅納斯達克綜合指數上漲
0.45% 至 27190.86，反觀標普 500 指數下跌 0.27% 至 7722.72；道瓊工業指數下跌 1.26% 至
51176.96。
9 月新增非農就業大幅走降，殖利率持續高檔

30 年期國債回購操作規模此前單次上限為 60 億美元，上一次僅接受 40.8 億美元，並於週四、週五
接力進行 10 年期與 30 年期國債競拍。此外，聯準會、歐洲央行將於週四公布 9 月貨幣政策會議紀

核心消費

公用事業
房地產
 9 月 FOMC 如預期升息一碼，儘管在消除不確定下，公債殖利率仍維持在高檔 。同時市場正在持續關注大型科技類股資本支出及其營收的效率 ，根據
Factset 資料預估 Q3 財報預估標普 500 每股盈餘年增為 27%，相比 Q2 的 33% 有所放緩，但 AI 基礎建設與雲端巨頭的強勁表現仍使科技股為大盤上漲
的主要動能，同時即將邁入多為上漲的第四季及期中選舉關鍵前夕，配合當下經濟數據仍具備穩健性質，可期待上漲行情仍能持續。然而，由於 10 月初

已顯漲幅集中於科技股，導致市場廣度的低迷，建議美股 Q3 財報前可部分獲利了結近期勁漲之半導體相關族群，以應對財報可能帶來的波動，等待分批
布局之機會，避開其餘利率敏感型族群。另外也同時部分握有基本面穩健之醫療保健類族群同步抗波動，消費族群因高通膨、利率近期相對承壓。
 歐洲各國的通膨進一步走高，然同步法國債信惡化下，近期升息步調可能暫緩，對於股市來說仍是中性看待；日本方面 8 月東京 CPI 也反彈至 1.9%，同
時電費與燃氣等補貼至 9 月已結束，10 月通膨數據將因價格調整下反彈，同步薪資通膨以及日圓貶勢未完全反轉下，通膨風險仍大，儘管 10 月升息預
期已轉為按兵不動，股市也同樣中性看待。整體而言日、歐股市在短線上須謹慎，需等待通膨與債市壓力等因子和緩後，長線仍看好。債券方面，目前公
司債信用利差走高有限，代表信用風險仍然可控，適合長期鎖利率布局投資等級公司債；採取中長天期搭配短天期的配置。總結以上，第一，慎選財報優
異族群，以科技為首，醫療為防禦配置；第二，日、歐因近期通膨壓力而轉為中性看待，長線看好；第三，布局投資等級債來進行長期鎖利配置。

ADP新增就業人數(3MA) 非農總私部門新增就業人數(3MA)
截止至 9 月美國當前私部門就業仍呈現偏弱勢走勢
(千人)
美國專欄
 美國 9 月份新增非農就業人數僅為 2.9 萬人，低於市場所預期的 8.9 萬人，且 7 月與
8 月的就業數據合計大幅下修 6.0 萬人。失業率由 4.1% 微升至 4.2%，因新增 48.5
萬人進入勞動市場，亦推升勞動參與率 0.2 %至 61.8%，邊際勞工數量也由 146.8 萬
人創兩年新低，整體顯就業在放緩中又帶有勞動力的結構恢復。另外，平均每小時薪
資年增由 3.1% 放緩至 3.0%，持續降溫無礙核心物價，整體削弱 10 月升息預期。

 產業方面服務業仍是主要支撐 ，以教育和保健服務為首(+2萬)，休閒酒店(+1萬)、零
售(+5.8千人)、運輸倉儲(+7.6千人)等項目也仍在成長，但金融(-7千人)、科技(-1萬)
產業就業持續受AI衝擊而走降。另外由於政府部門近期波動較大 ，因此觀察官方非農
與小非農 ADP 新增就業人數的3個月移動平均，相較疫情前水準雖偏弱，但仍維持
不陷入衰退的格局，配合失業率仍處低檔，顯勞動市場依舊接近充分就業狀態。

核心 PCE(YoY)  PCE(YoY) 超級核心 PCE (YoY)
 美國 8 月 PCE 物價年增持平於 3.4%，低於預期的 3.7%，同時核心物價年增也維持在 3.0%，低於預期的 3.3%，其中能源商品與服務年增達 16.8% 為

PCE 物價年增支撐的原因，然核心項目走降很大程度受益於 BEA 針對電腦軟體與配件、法律服務、投資組合管理服務三個細項統計方法的年度修正，這
次調整為核心通膨直接扣減了約 0.2 至 0.3 個百分點。總歸來看低於預期的數據發布後，市場對聯準會 10 月升息機率由逾５成降至約３成。然而在排
除能源影響下，物價仍保持一定僵固性，且 8 月 PCE 仍遠高於 2%，後續油價是通膨走降關鍵，並且留意核心項目的發展。
 細項方面，商品中的耐久財成長 0.1%，項目整體月增也沒有較前月明顯成長 ，僅由基期較低休閒娛樂品與 車輛年增反應較顯著；非耐久財月增由 -
0.4% 轉為 0.5%，年增由 3.3% 至 3.6%，由於 8 月國際油價再度上漲，非耐久財汽油及能源商品價格大漲 4.4%，年增也達到 27.9%；服務方面也由於
油價因素，交通運輸服務年增達 8 %，同時考慮到其餘服務相關物價指數項目，在 8 月多持平或走揚，因此觀察細項中排除非營利機構的服務家庭消費
支出月年增則上漲 0.1% 至 3.5%，顯示服務項目的僵固性仍存續。

8
美國 8 月實質個人消費支出強勁，第二季 GDP 因消費投資而上修美國專欄
 美國 8 月實質消費支出成長 0.55%，顯示消費動能依然強勁；然而實質
可支配所得成長則放緩至負成長，儲蓄率降至 4.1%，創近四年低位，凸
顯家庭支出依賴財務緩衝的消耗。即便如此，消費仍為經濟提供支撐 ，
使第二季 GDP 終值上修至 2.2%，其中消費貢獻由 2.12pp 升至2.51pp
為最大宗，投資也有次要的貢獻，最終修正也達 0.82pp。
 此背景下，8 月強勁的消費表現可望為第三季 GDP 的支撐力量，延續成
長動能，然而當前儲蓄率過低則須留意 消費緩衝能力下降、以及後續消
費動能有限的問題。

 債市回顧：上週美國國債全線承壓收跌。雖有紐約聯儲主席 John Williams 與副主席 Phillip Jefferson 釋出「無需急於行動(升息)」的偏鴿發言，加上
8 月 PCE 及 9 月非農就業疲軟帶動市場對 10 月暫停升息的預期升溫，一度推動殖利率盤中回落，但 12 月升息機率反而走升。另外，在川普政府施壓
下 G7 同意釋放 1 億桶油儲以對沖油價波動，但由於通膨仍顯著高於 2.0% 目標，且同時歐債帶來的壓力也持續共振全球，10 年期法德利差在週五已至
2012 年以來最大，因此原油敘事使債市帶來的反彈仍被逆轉，財政擔憂與期限議價仍持續拉抬殖利率。整週來看，2 年期美債殖利率累計下跌 2 個基
點至 4.82%，10 年期美債殖利率則攀升 11 個基點至 5.27%，30 年期利率更一度觸及 5.62% 的 20 年以來新高。

(2026/10/2) (目前利率區間)
(目前利率區間)
 短期展望：目前勞動市場穩健、通膨可控，經濟數據強勁下，市場普遍認為 12 月仍有一碼的空間，而非 10 月連續升息。而在此背景下，若油價無進
一步拉抬，本週 10 年期美債殖利率維持高檔震盪。而短端利率因升息預期逐步收斂未再顯著走升；相對地，長端的升幅較大，主要反映市場對更高長
期利率與期限溢酬的定價，但也非意味後續升息幅度必然更大。

歐洲專欄
 10 月 1 日法國總理勒科爾尼提出了 2027 年財政預算草案，計畫藉由約 430 億歐元的開支縮減與稅收調整，將預算赤字佔 GDP 比例從今年的近 5.5% 壓低

至 5.0%，以避免赤字恐飆破 6.5% 的失控局面。然而，即使該計畫順利落地，法國政府總債務占 GDP 比例仍將突破 120%，使官方宣稱在 2029 年將赤字
壓至 3% 的目標可能不切實際。另外，由於少數派政府在國民議會缺乏絕對多數，加上 2027 年春季總統大選將至、政壇極端勢力如極右翼國民聯盟（RN）
勒龐與極左翼不屈法國（LFI）梅朗雄民調持續高漲 (皆有望通過第一輪投票)，該預算極難在年底前獲得正常通過，恐迫使政府動用憲法手段強行過關並面臨
不信任案挑戰；在政治癱瘓與財政脆弱的雙重夾擊下，市場避險情緒驅使資金湧向德債，法德國公債利差短期可能突破 150 個基點。
 歐元區 9 月 HICP 初值年增率攀升 0.6 至 3.8%，創下三年新高且高於市場預期，反映中東局勢引發的能源價格衝擊；然而，法國財政動盪已促使市場金融條
件緊縮，歐洲央行預計本月不會急於升息，預計利率按兵不動，且在議會分裂與未承諾更積極財政調整的前提下，歐洲央行也難以直接啟動 TPI（傳導保護
工具）干預救市。儘管歐盟宣布釋放戰略柴油儲備暫時穩住了市場情緒，帶動歐元區公債與英國公債在尾盤回穩反彈，但仍無法扭轉短線上的基本面弱勢。

日本專欄 日央 9 月紀要偏鷹，短觀製造強服務弱，通膨預期緩和升息預期
 日本央行 9 月貨幣政策會議紀要顯示，多數委員認為需要持續升息以應對壓力，甚至有人主張加快步伐，整體顯鷹派態度。此次會議以 7 比 2 通過升
息，利率升至1995年以來最高，距離 6 月升息僅三個月，是自 1990 年以來最短間隔。背景是日本通膨環境出現改變，薪資增長、服務價格及進口成
本推動通膨接近2%，央行更關注能源價格、日圓走勢及企業定價行為。然而，央行內部也有意見認為應逐步升息，避免過於急迫，作為平衡考量。
 最新 9 月的短觀調查顯示全產業信心指數由 18 升至 21，主要受大型製造業帶動，該指數由 22 升至 24，創八年新高，反映全球 AI 需求強勁及供應

瓶頸緩解。相較之下，大型非製造業因原物料與人力成本上升 、消費者抗拒漲價，以及颱風豪雨衝擊餐飲旅宿業，指數微幅下滑至 35。另外大型產
業資本支出計畫年增率小幅下修 0.2 個百分點至 11.3%，但仍維持高檔，中小企業信心亦持續改善。企業對未來一年通膨預期下降至約 2.6%，三年
期則維持在 2.6%，顯示雖壓力仍高於央行目標，但未進一步升溫，降低了 10 月立即升息的急迫性，然而 12 月再度升息的可能性仍相對偏高。

 美光營收年增 379% 至 542.3 億美元，毛利率 87%
美光(MU) 2026 4Q 營收年增 379.3% 至 542.3億美元，遠超預期的 515.1 億美元。本季毛利率為 86.8%；EPS 32.87 美元，優於預期的 31.94 美元。DRAM 營
收 398 億美元，約占 73%；NAND 營收 141 億美元，占 26%，價格上漲幅度約 30%。資料中心 SSD 營收接近 100 億美元，占 NAND 營收近 2/3。
13
MU 美光財報遠超預期，2027 大部分供給量已定調
資料來源：Bloomberg、美光、聯邦整理
美光自由現金流預期並未因支出增加而受明顯影響美光營收指引穩定，毛利率維持 86% 水準

 下一季營收指引中位數約 615 億美元，毛利率預期為 86.25% (預期為全年毛利率低點)； 2027 全年資本支出、自由現金流皆將增加
公司預期 2027 1Q 營收中位數為 615 億美元，遠超市場預期的 570 億美元，而毛利率約為 86.25%，大致符合 86.7% 的市場預期，美光預期 2027 1Q 毛利率
為未來一年低點。並表示未來兩年 DRAM、NAND 市場供需將較 2026 年近一步收緊，2027 年大部分 HBM 供給已完成協議簽署，價格較 2026 年顯著提升，
縮小 HBM 與傳統 DRAM 毛利率差距；HBM 需求增速預期至 2028 年持續高於傳統 DRAM。公司已將 2027 年度資本支出指引調升為 500 億美元以上，主要
用於無塵室建設，為 2028 年後的需求作準備。

高資產財富顧問中心
 美光本季財報呈現標準且罕見的景氣完全爆發特徵
1. 遞延收入大幅上升：顯示客戶願意提前付款預定產能，營收能見度與保障度提高。
2. 營業活動現金流增速暴衝，且遠超營收與應收、應付增速：客戶預付款有效轉化
為實體營收，且收款能力增強。
3. 存貨中半成品與原料跳升，完成品庫存量持續下降：產品需求遠大於供給，完成
品無須存放便交與客戶。顯示市場供不應求情況加劇，公司產能滿載運轉。
4. 現金轉換循環驟降，且在遞延收入大增情況下，應收增速仍大於應付增速：銷貨
與產能周轉度極高，壓低存貨天數。且出貨量持續提升，放量迅速，客戶端待結
算金額迅速累積良性推升應收帳款增速。

營收(調整) 毛利率
 埃森哲業績、訂單增速皆超預期；大型數位、AI 轉型成為重要動能
埃森哲(ACN) 第四季業績全面超預期，營收年增 6% 至 186.8 億美元，超過市場預期 3.5% 也超出公司自身指引上緣。全年營收年增 5% 至 742 億美元；新訂單
年增 4% 至 220 億美元，超出市場預期約 11%，也相較前一年的年減 3% 明顯改善。2026 年累積訂單創紀錄達 845 億美元，並有 141 筆至少 1 億美元訂單。

此次財報緩解市場對「AI是否影響 IT 諮詢、軟體開發、外包服務需求」疑慮，執行長 Julie Sweet 表示大型企業如 FedEX 等正利用埃森哲來構建自身 AI 基礎設
施，反催生相關的新需求。
15
ACN 埃森哲財報 - AI 與軟體並非零和賽局
資料來源：Bloomberg、埃森哲、聯邦整理
埃森哲預期 2027 全年自由現金流穩步成長，並未因 AI 影響而承壓
(十億美元)
埃森哲預期明年營收個位數增長，全年毛利率略升
 2027 年營收指引預計年增 3 ~ 6%，已考量不利因素；確立 AI 仍可創造新需求
2027 財年營收預計成長 3~ 6%，其中約 2% 為併購所提攜，而中東地區業務持續承壓，全年估計約影響 10 億美元營收，該因素已於指引中排除。併購 50 億
金額將明確聚焦高成長領域（如 AI、數據、數據中心、OT 安全等），以加速長期有機成長。公司表示指引基本面雖穩健但行業競爭激烈，業務規模雖成長但多
線業務價格小幅下滑。本次業績指引提升市場對 IT 諮詢產業的展望，證實 AI 促進各產業轉型，加速基礎設施建設可創造新市場需求。

## Document 672: 酷暑の先に厳冬リスクを抱える欧州経済

Source PDF: all report pdf/酷暑の先に厳冬リスクを抱える欧州経済.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-09-09
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 9 月 9 日

MURC Focus

酷暑の先に厳冬リスクを抱える欧州経済

～過去最低水準の天然ガス貯蔵率で冬を迎える～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 堅調だった 4～6 月期の実質 GDP 
5 月以降、欧州は酷暑に見舞われており、さまざまな経済活動が圧迫されている。一方、欧州連合（EU）の 4
～6 月期の実質 GDP（国内総生産）の確定値は前期比0.6%増と、1～3 月期（同 0.1%増）から増勢が加速した。
主要な需要項目別には、内需は個人消費が増勢を加速させたほか、総固定資本形成が増加に転じ た。また外
需（純輸出）も、輸出のけん引を受けて大幅に反発し、景気を押し上げた。

ユーロ高基調にもかかわらず 4～6 月期の輸出が堅調だった理由としては、いわゆる“トランプ関税”の影響の
一巡があると考えられる。事実、名目輸出の動きを仕向け地別に確認すると、対米輸出が着実に回復しているこ
とがうかがい知れる。それ以外には、非 EU 欧州向けが底堅く増加していることが特徴的である。具体的には英
国やスイスといった国々向けの輸出である。 
図表 1. EU27 カ国の実質 GDP（確定値） 図表 2. EU27 カ国の EU 域外名目輸出

（出所）欧州連合統計局（ユーロスタット）

（注 1）季節調整前の原系列で作成。 
（注 2）その他のうち目立つものは中南米向け、カナダ向けなど。 
（出所）ユーロスタット

EU外
（前期差、10億ユーロ）
〇EU の4～6月期の輸出の堅調は技術的な性格によるところが大きく、その意味で実質 GDP の成長加速も過
大評価されている可能性が意識される。 
〇先行きの EU 経済を展望すると、年末年始にかけては、厳冬に伴うエネルギーインフレの深刻化で、景気が
下振れするリスクがある。 
〇EU はこの間、第三国からの調達を増やしてきたが、それでも 天然ガス貯蔵率は歴史的低水準にある。イラ
ン発のエネルギーショックの下で天然ガスの脱ロシア化を敢行したことの代償は大きい。

### Page 2

なおその他の仕向け地のうち、欧州内で目立つ先としては EU の加盟候補国（西バルカン諸国・トルコ）向けが
ある。一方、欧州外では、トランプ関税の発効 など米国の保護主義政策の台頭を警戒し、相互で貿易の拡大で
合意に達した国または地域との貿易の拡大が目立つ。具体的には 、カナダや中南米（メルコスール）、オーストラ
リア向けなどである。

このように分解すると、4～6 月期に堅調だった輸出だが、対米輸出はテクニカルな側面での反発の可能性が
高い。さらに非 EU 欧州向けの輸出だが、これは EU を含めた欧州経済全体の内需の底堅さを反映したもので
あり、必ずしも“外需”とは言えない性格を持つ。したがって、4～6 月期の輸出の堅調は技術的な性格によるとこ
ろが大きく、その意味で実質 GDP の成長加速も過大評価されている可能性がある。

2. 先行きに待ち構える厳冬に伴うエネルギーインフレリスク 
先行きの EU 経済を展望すると、年末年始にかけては、厳冬に伴うエネルギーインフレの深刻化で、景気が下
振れするリスクがある。EU27 カ国の消費者物価は直近 7 月時点で前年比 3.0%上昇と強含んでいる。2 月末に
生じたイラン発のエネルギーショックを受けて欧州のエネルギー価格も高騰し ており、それがインフレの加速につ
ながっている。

一方、EU の天然ガス貯蔵率は 9 月 7 日時点で 66.9%と、過去最低水準である（図表 3）。今年の EU の天然
ガス貯蔵率はそもそも年初から歴史的な低水準だったが、EU はイラン発のエネルギーショックにもかかわらず、
脱ロシアの観点からロシア産天然ガスの輸入を絞り込んだ。具体的には、短期契約による液化天然ガス（LNG ）
の輸入を 4 月 25 日から、パイプライン経由での天然ガス輸入を 6 月 17 日から、それぞれ禁じた。

図表 3. EU の天然ガス貯蔵率 図表 4. 欧州の天然ガス価格（オランダ TTF 先物）

### Page 3

EU は米国産 LNG を中心に第三国からの天然ガスの調達に努めてきたが、12 月頭までに 80%とする今年の
目標を達成することなく本格的な需要期である冬に突入することが ほぼ確実な情勢となった。暖冬であれば天然
ガス需要が減退すると期待されるが、厳冬の場合は需要が刺激されるため天然ガス価格は高騰を余儀なくされる。
そうなれば、欧州のインフレが一段と上振れすることになる。

すでに年末にかけては、今夏の酷暑に伴う農作物の不作を受けて食品インフレが顕在化する恐れが強まって
いる。さらに厳冬となれば、ガス需給が ひっ迫し、エネルギーインフレ がさらに強まることになる。 現在、太平洋で
歴史的な規模の“スーパー・エルニーニョ”が発達しており、この現象のもとで今夏の酷暑は生じた。 今冬は基本
的に暖かいと予想されているものの、冬半ば以降は厳しい寒波が到来すると予想されている。

なお欧州の天然ガス価格の指標であるオランダ TTF 先物（図表 4）は上昇トレンドで推移しており、9 月の初週
にはメガワット時当たり 70 ユーロ台に突入した。このガス価格の上昇は、今冬における天然ガスの需給ひっ迫を
織り込んでいるためだと考えられる。天然ガス価格の水準はロシア発のエネルギーショックの際に比べるとまだ低
いが、今後は一段と上昇する可能性が高い。

3. 相応に大きくなりそうな脱ロシアの代償 
今年に入ってからも EU は、対ロ制裁を次々と強化しており、今秋には第 21 次パッケージ（制裁対象企業の
拡大やロシア産原油の価格キャップ制度の厳格化など）の実行が予定されている。このように EU がロシアへの
圧力を強め続ける姿勢を堅持する以上、ロシア産天然ガスの輸入再開は見込めない。ロシアも外貨不足に陥っ
ているが、EU の高圧的な姿勢に鑑みれば、正規ルートでのガス輸出に応じるかよくわからない。

EU はこの間、第三国からの調達を増やしてきたが、それでも 天然ガス貯蔵率は歴史的低水準にある。イラン
発のエネルギーショックの下で天然ガスの脱ロシア化を敢行したことの代償は大きいと言わざるを得ない。天然ガ
スは主に家庭用のコジェネレーション（熱電併給）の燃料として利用されているため、厳冬となった場合、EU の市
民生活を直撃すると懸念される。
